```json
      "result": "{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"path\": \"/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_4/projects/trace-verdict-study/paper_framing.md\", \"content\": \"# Paper Framing — trace-verdict-study\\n\\n> v3.0 — [UPDATED 2026-05-15] D030 CLOSED: DeepSeek VOTA 84.45% confirmed correct (confirmatory re-eval). **Critical narrative fix**: to_chat_text() preserves labels → VOTA 训练时 verdict label 未被截断 → 论文中 \\\"label truncation/corruption\\\" 措辞对 VOTA 不成立。正确叙事: trace INPUT content 被截断 (91.7% samples), 84.45% 反映 trace information loss。新增 D11: VOTA cross-model gap confounded by training-time truncation。数据集来源：HumanEval (164) + MBPP-sanitized (427) = 591 problems × 10 LLM-generated solutions = 5910 samples。\\n\\n## Target Venue\\n- Primary: EMNLP 2026 Main (ARR submission)\\n- Fallback: EMNLP 2026 Findings\\n- Deadline: ARR May 25, 2026\\n\\n## Core Insight\\n\\nVerdict prediction (pass/fail) is fundamentally a **fault detection** task. The most effective signal is the **exception event** — a compact, structured diagnostic moment that consistently outperforms all other trace representations across models (Qwen 99.83% ± 0.00 across 3 seeds; DeepSeek 91-99% depending on seed, but always > VOTA 84.45% and no_trace 73.36%). In contrast, richer trace representations that require deeper execution reasoning show model-dependent effectiveness: VOTA gap = 14.96pp, no_trace gap = 1.77pp. Information density — not training signal distribution — is the primary bottleneck: even oracle loss masking (label-only) cannot close the 13pp gap between full traces and targeted extraction. The design principle: **extract the highest-specificity diagnostic signal; discard everything else.** Cross-model absolute performance depends on LoRA initialization stability, but exception_only's relative dominance over other components is robust.\\n\\nVOTA's primary contribution is **fault indicator isolation** — automatically extracting exception events from noisy full traces — rather than generic trace compression. The \\\"compression helps\\\" narrative was a stepping stone; the real mechanism is diagnostic signal extraction.\\n\\n## Core Claims (3)\\n\\n### Claim 1: Exception events are the most effective trace component for verdict prediction\\n\\nException summaries consistently achieve the highest verdict prediction accuracy across both models tested, outperforming all other trace representations in every seed. On Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B, performance is perfectly robust: 99.83% ± 0.00 (3 seeds). On DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B, absolute accuracy is seed-dependent (seed42=99.83% epoch1, seed123=91.43% epoch1; Δ=8.40pp), consistent with LoRA initialization sensitivity — but exception_only still dominates VOTA (84.45%) and no_trace (73.36%) in all seeds. This contrasts with VOTA (cross-model gap = 14.96pp) and no_trace (gap = 1.77pp). Exception events are structurally simple (`[ERROR] type: ... message: ...`) and do not require deep trace reasoning.\\n\\n**Supporting evidence:**\\n- `vota_exception_only_seed42_v2`: Qwen 99.83%, ECE=0.0027\\n- `deepseek_exception_only_seed42`: DeepSeek 99.83% (epoch1) / 99.33% (epoch3)\\n- `deepseek_exception_only_seed456`: DeepSeek 99.33% (epoch3), CI [98.82, 99.75] — 与 seed42 完全一致\\n- `deepseek_exception_only_seed123`: DeepSeek 91.43% (epoch1) / 91.09% (epoch3) — LoRA init sensitivity outlier\\n- `deepseek_vota_seed42`: DeepSeek VOTA 84.45% (gap = 14.96pp vs Qwen)\\n- `deepseek_no_trace_seed42`: DeepSeek no_trace 73.36% (gap = 1.77pp vs Qwen)\\n- `vota_no_ae_seed42`: 99.41% — actual/expected removal 不降，非 trivial string comparison\\n\\n**DeepSeek seed analysis** ✅ (FINALIZED, D029):\\n\\n| Seed | Epoch 3 (final) | fail_recall | pass_recall | train_loss | Errors | CI |\\n|------|-----------------|-------------|-------------|------------|--------|-----|\\n| 42   | 99.33%          | 1.0         | 0.9906      | —          | 8/1190 | — |\\n| 456  | 99.33%          | 1.0         | 0.9906      | 0.1225     | 8/1190 | [98.82, 99.75] |\\n| 123  | 91.09%          | 0.705       | 0.993       | 0.122      | —      | [89.4, 92.6] |\\n\\nseed42 与 seed456 结果**完全一致**（均 8/1190 错误，99.33%），seed123 为离群值。seed123 的 fail_recall 仅 0.705 — LoRA 初始化落入差的局部最优，未充分学到 exception→fail 映射。**关键发现**: seed456 和 seed123 的 train_loss 几乎相同（0.1225 vs 0.122），但 accuracy 差 8.2pp — token-level loss 掩盖了 sample-level 分类能力的巨大差异（详见 D10）。\\n\\n**Irreducible error analysis** ✅: 3 seeds 错误的 2 个样本 (id=901, 905) 均来自 HumanEval/95 (`check_dict_case`)，根因为 Python `all()` 对 generator expression 短路触发 `GeneratorExit`（`BaseException` 子类，非真正错误）。tracer 将其标记为 `[ERROR]`，exception_only 模型看到 7 行 `[ERROR] GeneratorExit`，合理但错误地预测 fail。整个 1190 条测试集仅这 2 条含 `GeneratorExit`，模型无训练信号学习其良性本质。**这确认 99.83% 是 exception_only 表征的理论上限，剩余 0.17% 是 representation-inherent irreducible error，而非模型不足。**\\n\\n**Seed robustness** ✅: Qwen exception_only 3-seed 完全一致，构成本文最强 robustness 证据：\\n\\n| Seed | Accuracy | 95% CI | Errors |\\n|------|----------|--------|--------|\\n| 42 | 99.83% | [99.58, 100] | 2/1190 |\\n| 123 | 99.83% | [99.58, 100] | 2/1190 |\\n| 456 | 99.83% | [99.58, 100] | 2/1190 |\\n\\n3 seeds 错误样本 100% 重叠（id=901, 905, 均为 `check_dict_case` 函数, label=pass → pred=fail）。**Irreducible samples**: 模型已达数据集理论上限，variance = 0。\\n\\n**DeepSeek cross-model** ✅ (D029): 3-seed 完整数据 — seed42/456=99.33%, seed123=91.09%。2/3 seeds 鲁棒，seed123 为 LoRA 初始化离群值（fail_recall 仅 0.705）。Qwen 3-seed 完美稳定 (99.83% ± 0.00) vs DeepSeek 高方差（σ≈4.8pp），暗示 exception signal 的 learnability 与模型 LoRA 训练稳定性相关。但即使最差 seed (91.09%) 仍优于 VOTA (84.45%) 和 no_trace (73.36%)，relative dominance robust。\\n\\n### Claim 2: Information density, not gradient dilution, drives trace compression benefit\\n\\nFull traces help verdict prediction (81.76% [`full_trace_seed42_v5`], +6.6pp over baseline), but VOTA achieves 99.41% [`vota_seed42_v5`] (+24.3pp) — a +17.6pp advantage. Label-only loss ablation (plan_002) quantifies the causal decomposition: **gradient dilution contributes ~26% of the gap** (4.5pp; label-only full_trace = 86.22% [`full_trace_label_only_seed42`]), **information overload contributes ~74%** (13.0pp; only trace compression resolves this). Even with oracle loss masking that eliminates gradient dilution, full traces still underperform VOTA by 13pp.\\n\\nCross-model evidence reinforces this: VOTA gap (14.96pp) partly tracks base model execution reasoning capability (Qwen CRUXEval-I 56.5% vs DeepSeek 39.0%, Δ=17.5pp), though **confounded by DeepSeek training-time trace truncation** (91.7% of VOTA samples truncated to ~2048 tokens; see D11). The two factors cannot be disentangled in the current design. Exception signal partially bypasses both bottlenecks (token-short, no truncation; consistently best component on both models), though DeepSeek shows seed-dependent absolute performance (91-99%).\\n\\n**Supporting evidence:**\\n- `full_trace_label_only_seed42`: 86.22%, ECE=0.1107 (gradient dilution fix, +4.5pp over standard full_trace)\\n- `full_trace_seed42_v5`: 81.76%, ECE=0.0958 (standard full_trace)\\n- `vota_seed42_v5`: 99.41%, ECE=0.0054\\n- `deepseek_vota_seed42`: 84.45% (VOTA model-dependent)\\n- CRUXEval literature data (Wang et al.)\\n\\n### Claim 3: Two-tier diagnostic hierarchy for verdict prediction\\n\\nComponent ablation reveals verdict-relevant trace information has a strict two-tier hierarchy:\\n- **Tier 1 — diagnostic moment signals**: exception summaries (99.83% [`vota_exception_only_seed42_v2`]) and assertion-point information (99.92% [`vota_ae_only_seed42`]) are individually near-perfect and sufficient for verdict prediction on Qwen. These signals are structurally simple and consistently the most effective component on both models, though DeepSeek absolute performance is seed-dependent.\\n- **Tier 2 — behavioral patterns**: loop compression (85.63% [`vota_loop_only_seed42`]) carries substantial signal but is model-dependent and fully subsumed by Tier 1.\\n\\nCombining tiers introduces noise rather than helping: exception_only (99.83%) > VOTA full (99.41%), p=0.01. Loop_only ≈ full_trace_label_only (85.63% vs 86.22%) confirms VOTA's loop compression precisely extracts the useful behavioral signal from full traces.\\n\\n**Supporting evidence:**\\n- `vota_exception_only_seed42_v2`: 99.83%, ECE=0.0027 (Tier 1 exception)\\n- `vota_ae_only_seed42`: 99.92%, ECE=0.0004 (Tier 1 assertion-point, 与 exception 统计等价 p=0.80)\\n- `vota_loop_only_seed42`: 85.63%, ECE=0.0831 (Tier 2 behavioral)\\n- `full_trace_label_only_seed42`: 86.22%, ECE=0.1107 (≈ loop_only, confirming extraction equivalence)\\n- `deepseek_exception_only_seed42`: 99.83% epoch1 / 99.33% epoch3 (Tier 1 cross-model validation)\\n- `deepseek_vota_seed42`: 84.45% (Tier 1+2 cross-model: Tier 2 introduces model-dependent degradation)\\n\\n**Calibration**: 完整 calibration 数据确认 Tier 1 信号不仅 accuracy 近完美，置信度校准也近完美：\\n\\n| Condition | Accuracy | ECE | Brier | 解读 |\\n|-----------|----------|-----|-------|------|\\n| ae_only | 99.92% | 0.0004 | 0.0008 | 近乎完美校准 |\\n| exception_only | 99.83% | 0.0027 | 0.0013 | 近乎完美校准 |\\n| VOTA | 99.41% | 0.0054 | 0.005 | 优秀，但 Tier 2 引入轻微 noise |\\n| loop_only | 85.63% | 0.0831 | — | Tier 2 单独校准中等 |\\n| label_only | 86.22% | 0.1107 | — | gradient dilution fix 仍 miscalibrated |\\n| full_trace | 81.76% | 0.0958 | — | 中度 miscalibrated |\\n| no_trace | 75.13% | 0.1715 | 0.1972 | 严重 miscalibrated |\\n\\nno_trace ECE 是 exception_only 的 **~63x**（0.1715/0.0027），Brier 是 **~150x**（0.1972/0.0013）。Trace signal（尤其 Tier 1）不仅提升 accuracy，更从根本上改善模型 calibration。\\n\\n**Design principle**: verdict-optimized traces should extract highest-specificity diagnostic signals; lower-specificity signals are redundant and may introduce noise.\\n\\n## Cross-Model Evidence Summary\\n\\n| Condition | Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B | DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B | Cross-model gap | Interpretation |\\n|-----------|-------------------|---------------------|-----------------|----------------|\\n| exception_only | 99.83% ± 0.00 (3 seeds) | seed42/456: 99.33%; seed123: 91.09% | 0.50-8.74pp (seed-dependent) | **Best component on both models**; 2/3 DeepSeek seeds match Qwen-level; 1 outlier still > VOTA |\\n| VOTA | 99.41% | 84.45% | -14.96pp | Model-dependent — requires execution reasoning; **also confounded by DeepSeek training-time trace truncation** (91.7% of VOTA samples truncated from mean 2626 to ~2048 tokens; labels preserved via to_chat_text(), see D11) |\\n| no_trace | 75.13% | 73.36% | -1.77pp | Baseline gap tracks general capability |\\n| full_trace | 81.76% | collapsed (v3 pending) | N/A | DeepSeek v1 label truncation bug |\\n\\n**Key insight**: Exception_only is consistently the best component on both models (even worst DeepSeek seed 91.09% > VOTA 84.45%). Cross-model gap 与 signal complexity 正相关, but exception signal's absolute effectiveness also depends on LoRA training stability. Qwen: perfectly robust (3 seeds identical). DeepSeek: 2/3 seeds at 99.33% (matching Qwen-level), 1 outlier at 91.09% (LoRA initialization). Median performance robust; seed sensitivity suggests smaller/weaker models may need training stabilization (multi-seed selection, warmup tuning) for optimal exception signal utilization.\\n\\n## VOTA Repositioning\\n\\n**旧叙事**: VOTA 是 trace compression 方法，价值在于\\\"压缩使 trace 更有效\\\"。\\n\\n**新叙事**: VOTA 是 **fault indicator isolation** 方法，核心价值在于自动从 noisy execution traces 中提取 exception events 等 diagnostic signals。\\n\\n**证据链**:\\n1. exception_only (99.83%) ≥ VOTA full (99.41%) — 压缩后的其他组件（loop, ae）不仅无益，反而引入 noise\\n2. VOTA 跨模型 gap = 14.96pp — 因为 VOTA 包含 model-dependent 的 Tier 2 组件（loop compression 需要 execution reasoning）\\n3. exception_only 在两个模型上均为最优组件 — isolate 出 Tier 1 后，相对优势 robust（虽然 DeepSeek 绝对值 seed-dependent）\\n4. vota_no_ae (99.41%) = VOTA (99.41%) — actual/expected 移除无影响，exception 是真正的 active ingredient\\n\\n**论文中的表述**: \\\"VOTA's trace compression pipeline incidentally isolates exception events — the minimal sufficient diagnostic signal. Its value lies not in making traces shorter, but in separating high-specificity fault indicators from low-specificity behavioral patterns.\\\"\\n\\n**对 Method section 的影响**: VOTA 仍然完整描述（loop compression + exception + actual/expected），但 Results/Discussion 明确指出 exception isolation 是核心机制，其他组件是 model-capability-dependent bonus。\\n\\n## Story Spine\\n\\n- **Problem**: Existing trace SFT studies (DCSH, TracePile) evaluate only generative tasks and find limited, model-dependent benefit. No prior work has tested trace SFT on verdict tasks (binary pass/fail), nor identified what trace components drive classification performance.\\n\\n- **Existing approaches & gaps**: DCSH (EMNLP Findings 2025) tested 5 trace formats on generation tasks, finding that \\\"no single trace representation consistently outperforms others.\\\" TracePile (NeurIPS 2025) scaled to 2.6M samples but only evaluated generation. TRACED (ICSE 2024) showed traces help encoder-based classification. All existing trace formats are designed for execution simulation, not fault detection.\\n\\n- **Our framing**: We reframe verdict prediction as **fault detection** and ask: what is the minimal sufficient diagnostic signal? Through systematic component ablation across two architecturally distinct models, we discover that exception events — compact, structured error summaries — are consistently the most effective trace component, achieving near-perfect accuracy on Qwen (99.83% ± 0.00, 3 seeds) and outperforming all alternatives on DeepSeek (91-99% vs VOTA 84.45%). Richer trace representations work only when the base model has sufficient execution reasoning capability, making them unreliable for general deployment.\\n\\n- **Headline result**: \\\"Exception events consistently achieve the highest verdict accuracy across models (Qwen 99.83%, DeepSeek 91-99%), outperforming VOTA traces (84.45% on DeepSeek, -14.96pp gap) — demonstrating that fault indicator isolation, not trace reasoning, is the robust mechanism for verdict prediction. DeepSeek seed sensitivity reveals LoRA training stability as a secondary factor for smaller models.\\\"\\n\\n## Story Arc\\n\\n1. **Setup**: Trace SFT for code tasks — promising but inconsistent in prior work (DCSH, TracePile). Unknown whether traces help verdict tasks and what trace design matters.\\n\\n2. **First result — traces help, compression helps more** (single model): no_trace 75.13% → full_trace 81.76% → VOTA 99.41%. Label-only loss ablation decomposes the gap: 26% gradient dilution, 74% information overload. Compression improves accuracy through higher information density, not just cost savings.\\n\\n3. **Twist — what component actually drives VOTA?** Component ablation reveals exception_only (99.83%) ≥ full VOTA (99.41%). The \\\"trace reasoning\\\" narrative is wrong — verdict prediction is fault detection. Tier 1 (exceptions + assertion-point) subsumes Tier 2 (loops). Actual/expected removal doesn't hurt (99.41%).\\n\\n4. **Cross-model stress test — consistent dominance, not universal perfection**: Exception_only is the best component on both models (even DeepSeek worst seed 91.09% > VOTA 84.45%), but absolute performance is seed-dependent on DeepSeek (91-99% vs Qwen's perfectly stable 99.83%). VOTA degrades by 14.96pp. The structurally simplest component is the most robust, but LoRA initialization sensitivity introduces variance on weaker models. Qwen seed robustness (3-seed variance = 0) + calibration (ECE=0.0027) 确认 exception signal 在强模型上的 reliability。DeepSeek seed sensitivity 揭示 training stability 作为 secondary bottleneck。\\n\\n5. **Design principle**: For verdict tasks, identify the highest-specificity diagnostic signal (exception events) and discard everything else. VOTA's value is fault indicator isolation — automatically extracting exceptions from noisy traces — not generic compression. Simpler signals are more robust; complexity tolerance depends on model capability.\\n\\n## Dataset Characteristics\\n\\n### Data duplication analysis\\n\\n| Condition | Total | Unique | Duplication rate | Cause |\\n|-----------|-------|--------|------------------|-------|\\n| exception_only_train | 4720 | 2858 | 39.45% | 同题多个 passing 解映射为相同 (input, output) — 无异常 → 同一 trace |\\n| exception_only_test | 1190 | 607 | 48.99% | 同上 |\\n| raw_traces | 5910 | 5803 | 1.81% | 完整 trace 含行号/变量值/内存地址，几乎不重复 |\\n\\n**Train-test input overlap: 0（无数据泄漏）**\\n\\n**解读**: exception_only 高重复率是 representation 的自然属性，非数据问题。多个正确解对同一测试用例执行时若均无异常，exception_only 表征退化为相同的空/无异常标记。这恰好说明 exception signal 的 compactness — 它仅编码 fault presence，不编码 solution identity。raw_traces 重复率极低 (1.81%) 因为完整 trace 包含 solution-specific 信息（变量值、内存地址）。尽管高重复率意味着有效训练样本少于名义数量，模型仍达 99.83% — 进一步确认 exception signal 的 learnability。\\n\\n## DeepSeek full_trace 处理\\n\\n**现状**: v1 坍塌（28.5%, 100% 预测 fail, 根因: 训练端 label 截断）。v2 重训启动但被 kill（Loop #6 ops worker 腾 GPU 给 vota_seed456）。v3 RUNNING（label-preserving truncation fix）。\\n\\n**处理方案**: v3 正在 cuda:0 上训练，预计 5/16 完成。\\n\\n**VOTA vs full_trace 截断机制区分** (D030 token analysis 确认):\\n- **full_trace v1**: SFTTrainer right-truncation 切掉了 verdict **label** → 100% predict fail → 坍塌。根因: DeepSeek tokenizer 对 full_trace 产生 >2048 tokens, label 在截断窗口外。\\n- **VOTA**: `to_chat_text()` 保留 verdict label（label 完整），但 91.7% 的样本 **trace content（input）被截断**（mean 2626 → ~2048 tokens）。84.45% 是真实结果，反映 trace 信息损失对模型学习的影响，**不是** label corruption。\\n- **exception_only**: token 长度远低于 2048，不受截断影响，跨模型对比 clean。\\n\\n**对论文叙事的影响**: 论文中所有 \\\"right-truncation corrupts/removes the verdict label\\\" 措辞**对 VOTA 不成立**——label 完整保留，被截断的是 trace input。正确表述: \\\"trace content truncation causes information loss\\\"。VOTA cross-model gap (14.96pp) 有两个贡献因素: (1) model capability, (2) training-time trace truncation on DeepSeek (see D11)。\\n\\n## Venue Fit Risks\\n\\n| Risk | Assessment | Mitigation |\\n|------|-----------|------------|\\n| 单任务 | 仍然只在 test verdict prediction 上验证 | 跨模型验证部分弥补; Discussion 中讨论 generalization |\\n| 单数据集 | HumanEval + MBPP-sanitized 是唯一数据源 (591 problems × 10 solutions) | 定位为 empirical study, 深度优于广度; 两个标准 benchmark 组合覆盖多样编程题型; exception_only 有效训练样本仅 2858 unique 即达 99.83%, 暗示 signal 的 simplicity 降低对数据量的依赖 |\\n| \\\"只是 feature engineering\\\" | exception extraction 不复杂 | simplicity 本身是 contribution — 最简信号最 robust |\\n| Negative result 味道 | VOTA 跨模型不 robust | Reframe: 识别 robust vs non-robust 组件边界是 positive |\\n| Seed robustness (Qwen) | ✅ 已解决: 99.83% ± 0.00 (3 seeds), irreducible samples 确认 | — |\\n| Seed robustness (DeepSeek) | ✅ 3-seed 完整: 2/3 at 99.33%, 1 at 91.09% | LoRA initialization sensitivity — median robust, worst still > all alternatives; 转化为 training stability finding (D9/D10) |\\n| Calibration | ✅ 完整数据: Tier 1 ECE≤0.003, no_trace ECE=0.171 (~63x gap) | Brier ~150x gap 进一步确认 |\\n| 只用 2 个模型 | 有限但有意义的 diversity | Architecture + pretraining data (5.5T vs 2T) + CRUXEval gap (17.5pp) 构成充分差异 |\\n| 数据重复率 | exception_only 训练集 39.45% 重复 | 非数据泄漏（train-test overlap = 0）；是 representation compactness 的自然结果；有效样本仍足够学习 |\\n\\n## Anticipated Reviewer Concerns\\n\\n| Concern | Planned Response |\\n|---------|-----------------|\\n| \\\"exception_only 99.83% 是不是 trivial pattern matching [ERROR] 有无？\\\" | full_trace 也包含 [ERROR] 但仅 81.76%——模型需要从 trace context 中 extract 和 utilize exception signal, 不是简单 keyword detection. no_trace 无 exception, 75.13%. |\\n| \\\"为什么 exception_only > VOTA? adding info 反而降?\\\" | 非 exception 组件 (loop/ae tokens) 稀释 attention 对 exception 信号的聚焦. Calibration: exception_only ECE=0.0027 vs VOTA ECE=0.0054. 部分样本 loop 正常但有 exception, 引入矛盾信号. |\\n| \\\"CRUXEval gap 与 VOTA gap 的相关性可能是 coincidence\\\" | 明确标注为 hypothesis. 列出 alternatives (tokenizer efficiency, pretraining scale 2.75x, chat template). 不 overclaim. |\\n| \\\"只有 2 个模型，universality claim 太强\\\" | 同意是 limitation. 两模型在 architecture (RoPE vs ALiBi), pretraining data (5.5T vs 2T), execution reasoning (CRUXEval 17.5pp gap) 上有充分差异, 不是 trivial replication. Exception signal 的 structural simplicity 提供 inductive argument for broader generalization. |\\n| \\\"99.83% 可能 saturated, 看不出 exception 到底学了什么\\\" | Calibration 双指标: exception_only ECE=0.0027, Brier=0.0013 (近完美校准). 对比 no_trace ECE=0.1715, Brier=0.1972 (~63x/~150x gap). **Irreducible error 根因已明确**: 2/1190 errors 均因 Python `all()` 短路触发 `GeneratorExit`（`BaseException` 子类，非真正错误），tracer 将其标记为 `[ERROR]`。模型看到 7 行 `[ERROR] GeneratorExit` 合理预测 fail，但代码实际正确。整个测试集仅这 2 条含 `GeneratorExit`，无训练信号可学习其良性本质。99.83% 是 representation 的理论上限，非 saturation artifact。 |\\n| \\\"只用了 1 个 seed\\\" | Qwen ✅: 3 seeds 99.83% ± 0.00. DeepSeek ✅: 3 seeds 完整 — seed42/456=99.33%, seed123=91.09%。2/3 鲁棒，1 离群。median=99.33%, worst=91.09% (仍 > VOTA 84.45%)。论文透明报告所有 seeds，将 seed sensitivity 转化为 LoRA training stability 的 finding（D9）。 |\\n| \\\"eval_loss 上升但 accuracy 提升——checkpoint selection 有问题?\\\" | Discussion D1: token-level loss 与 binary verdict accuracy 解耦. 推荐 verdict-specific metric. Methodological contribution. |\\n| \\\"DeepSeek full_trace 坍塌是 bug 不是 finding\\\" | 诚实披露根因 (训练端 label 截断). 其他 3 条件不受影响. 修复版本如就绪则补入. |\\n| \\\"calibration 数据只有 1 个 seed\\\" | Seed robustness 已证明 accuracy variance=0 (3 seeds), calibration 在 accuracy 完全一致的前提下 expected 也一致. ECE/Brier 的极小值 (0.0027/0.0013) 留有极小 variance 空间. |\\n| \\\"DeepSeek epoch1 > epoch3, 为什么用 epoch3 报告?\\\" | 正文报告所有 seeds 的 final + best checkpoint。seed42: epoch1=99.83%, epoch3=99.33%。seed123: epoch1=91.43%, epoch3=91.09%。D7 讨论 early stopping + D9 讨论 LoRA initialization sensitivity。不 cherry-pick best seed。 |\\n| \\\"DeepSeek seed sensitivity 说明 exception signal 不 robust\\\" | Exception_only 在所有 seeds 上仍优于其他条件（worst seed 91.09% > VOTA 84.45%）。Seed sensitivity 是 LoRA training 问题而非 signal 问题——Qwen 3 seeds 完美证明 signal 本身 robust。D9 讨论 training stabilization 作为 practical recommendation。 |\\n| \\\"exception_only 数据集 ~40-49% 重复，有效训练样本不足？\\\" | 重复源自 representation compactness：同题多个 passing 解均无异常 → 相同 trace。**非数据泄漏**（train-test input overlap = 0）。raw_traces 仅 1.81% 重复，确认重复是 exception_only 表征特性而非数据收集问题。模型在有效样本仅 2858 unique 的条件下仍达 99.83%，反而说明 exception signal 的高 learnability — 少量 unique 样本即可充分学习 fault detection pattern。 |\\n\\n## Discussion 要点\\n\\n### D1: eval_loss vs verdict accuracy 解耦\\nDeepSeek VOTA: eval_loss epoch1→3 从 0.068 升至 0.112 (+65%), 但 accuracy 从 78.15% 升至 84.45% (+6.3pp). Token-level calibration 与 binary classification accuracy 方向相反. **Implication**: verdict tasks 应用 task-specific metric (accuracy/F1) 做 checkpoint selection, 非 eval_loss.\\n\\n### D2: CRUXEval gap 假说 (revised — D030 confound)\\nVOTA 跨模型 gap (14.96pp) 与 CRUXEval execution reasoning gap (15-17.5pp) 数值吻合. 假说: trace utilization effectiveness 受 base model \\\"execution reasoning readiness\\\" 约束. **标注**: hypothesis, N=2, 列出 alternatives. 不 overclaim. **注意**: 我们的训练/测试数据来自 HumanEval + MBPP-sanitized（非 CRUXEval），CRUXEval-I 分数仅作为衡量两个模型 execution reasoning 能力差异的外部参照。论文中必须明确区分数据来源与外部基准引用。**D030/D034 truncation evidence**: 14.96pp gap 中截断效应有因果证据: group_analysis 显示 train-truncated 83.04% vs train-intact 100% (Fisher p≈0, 推理时无截断)。截断因果方向可 claim，模型能力差异是 residual confound。CRUXEval gap 数值吻合可能部分巧合。论文中截断效应应作为 established factor 讨论（有 group_analysis 因果证据），模型能力假说标注为 residual confound hypothesis。\\n\\n### D3: Simplicity as robustness (revised)\\nException signal 在两模型上均为最优组件，其结构简单性确保了相对优势的跨模型 robustness。但 DeepSeek seed sensitivity 揭示：simplicity 确保 signal 本身 robust，但 LoRA 训练过程是否稳定收敛到正确映射取决于模型。**trace design 应匹配 deployment model 的能力下界**，且需关注 training stability（建议：多 seed 验证 + 考虑 training stabilization 技巧如 warmup、learning rate schedule）。\\n\\n### D4: Checkpoint selection 与 gap 报告 (revised)\\n正文报告所有 seeds 的 final checkpoint 结果 + best checkpoint 结果，不 cherry-pick。DeepSeek seed42: epoch1=99.83%, epoch3=99.33%（退化 0.50pp）。DeepSeek seed123: epoch1=91.43%, epoch3=91.09%（退化 0.34pp）。两个 seed 均显示轻微 epoch 退化，但核心差异在 epoch1 起点（8.40pp gap），不是训练过程。\\n\\n### D5: Irreducible samples — GeneratorExit 根因分析\\n3 seeds 的 2/1190 errors 完全相同（id=901, 905, 均来自 HumanEval/95 `check_dict_case`），方向一致（pass→fail false negatives）。\\n\\n**根因**: Python `all()` 对 generator expression 短路时丢弃未耗尽的 generator，触发 `GeneratorExit`。`GeneratorExit` 继承自 `BaseException`（非 `Exception`），是 Python 内部控制流机制而非真正错误。但 tracer 对所有 caught exceptions 统一标记 `[ERROR]`，导致 exception_only 模型看到 7 行 `[ERROR] GeneratorExit`，合理但错误地预测 fail。\\n\\n**为何不可约**:\\n1. **表征局限**: exception_only 条件下模型仅见异常数据，无法访问控制流、返回值或 assertion 结果。`GeneratorExit` 在此表征中与真正错误不可区分。\\n2. **跨 seed 确定性**: 3 个 random seeds 产生完全相同的 errors，确认这是 data/representation 属性而非模型初始化随机性。\\n3. **训练集无信号**: 整个 1190 条测试集仅这 2 条含 `GeneratorExit`，训练集中也缺乏足够的 `GeneratorExit` 良性样本供模型学习。\\n4. **人类也会犯错**: 仅看 exception trace（7 行 `[ERROR]`），人类审查者在不了解 Python generator protocol 的情况下也会预测 fail。\\n\\n**对论文的意义**: (1) 99.83% 是 exception_only representation 在此数据集上的理论 ceiling，非 saturation artifact；(2) 揭示 tracer 设计的改进方向——区分 `BaseException` 子类（如 `GeneratorExit`, `KeyboardInterrupt`）与 `Exception` 子类（真正的程序错误）可消除此类 irreducible error；(3) 提供 concrete evidence 说明 exception_only 模型\\\"理解\\\"的是 `[ERROR]` tag 的 fault indicator 语义，而非 exception type 的 fine-grained 含义。\\n\\n### D6: Calibration as secondary evidence\\nCalibration 数据提供独立于 accuracy 的 evidence dimension。no_trace Brier=0.1972 说明模型无 trace 时不仅预测错，且对错误预测过度自信。exception_only Brier=0.0013 说明模型在有 exception signal 时几乎完美区分确定性和不确定性。这支持 Claim 1 的 \\\"sufficient signal\\\" 论点——exception 不仅让模型预测对，还让模型 \\\"知道自己对\\\"。\\n\\n### D7: Early stopping for exception signal (revised — D030 epoch1 data complete)\\n3-seed epoch1 完整数据：seed42 epoch1=99.83% → epoch3=99.33%（-0.50pp）；**seed456 epoch1=99.33% → epoch3=99.33%（0.00pp）**；seed123 epoch1=91.43% → epoch3=91.09%（-0.34pp）。**seed42 的 epoch1=99.83%（匹配 Qwen）是该 seed 独有的 LoRA 初始化运气，不是 DeepSeek epoch1 的通用属性。** seed456 epoch1=epoch3 说明 epoch 退化非普遍现象。核心差异仍是 LoRA 初始化（epoch1 起点差 8.40pp），不是 overfitting。Early stopping 无法挽救差的初始化（seed123）。**Implication**: (1) 报告 DeepSeek 结果时应使用 median/2-of-3 (99.33%)，不应以 seed42 epoch1=99.83% 作为 headline；(2) epoch1 peak 是 LoRA 初始化运气的副产物，不具推广性。对比: VOTA 在 DeepSeek 上 84.45% **已确认正确**（D030 关闭: confirmatory re-eval max_length=16384 → 84.45%，与原始训练评估精确一致；D030 根因是 evaluate.py v2 的 max_length=2048 eval-time 截断 bug 导致 82.6% 预测为 garbage tokens）。\\n\\n### D9: LoRA initialization sensitivity as secondary bottleneck [NEW — D028]\\nDeepSeek exception_only seed42=99.83% vs seed123=91.43% (epoch1), 8.40pp gap。Qwen 3 seeds 完全一致。假说：exception signal 的 learnability 取决于 (1) signal 本身的结构简单性（cross-model robust）+ (2) LoRA 初始化是否落入能正确映射 exception→verdict 的 basin。Qwen-7B 可能因模型更强/LoRA landscape 更平滑而对初始化不敏感。DeepSeek-6.7B（CRUXEval-I 39% vs Qwen 56.5%）的 loss landscape 可能更 rugged，LoRA 初始化更易陷入 suboptimal basin（seed123 的 fail_recall 仅 70-72% 说明模型未学到 exception→fail 映射的关键部分）。**论文角度**: 这不是 negative result——它揭示了 trace SFT 的 practical consideration（training stability），可作为 contribution 的一部分。建议 Discussion 讨论 training stabilization 策略（warmup, lr schedule, LoRA rank 调整, multi-seed selection）。\\n\\n### D10: Train loss ≈ but accuracy ≠ across seeds [NEW — D029]\\nDeepSeek exception_only seed456 (train_loss=0.1225) 与 seed123 (train_loss=0.122) 训练损失几乎相同，但 accuracy 差 8.2pp（99.33% vs 91.09%）。关键差异集中在 fail_recall（1.0 vs 0.705）——token-level loss 在所有 tokens 上平均，掩盖了 fail 类少数样本的 sample-level 分类能力差异。**Implication**: (1) 扩展 D1 (eval_loss vs accuracy decoupling) 到训练阶段：train_loss 同样不是 verdict task accuracy 的可靠代理指标；(2) 对 checkpoint selection 的实际建议更强：必须用 task-specific metric (accuracy/F1)，甚至 train_loss 收敛也不保证 task performance；(3) fail 类作为少数类（28.5%），其学习质量被平均 loss 稀释——这在 class-imbalanced 设置下是 general pattern，可推广到其他 verdict/detection 任务。\\n\\n### D11: VOTA cross-model gap: training-time trace truncation as causal factor [NEW — D030 token analysis + group_analysis]\\nD030 token analysis 确认: `to_chat_text()` 保留 labels（verdict label 在训练时未被截断），但 91.7% 的 DeepSeek VOTA 训练样本的 **trace content** 从 mean 2626 tokens 被截断到 ~2048 tokens。\\n\\n**Group analysis 提供因果证据**: 将测试集按训练截断状态分组（推理时无截断, max_length=16384），train-truncated 组 accuracy=83.04% vs train-intact 组 accuracy=100%, Fisher exact p≈0。**截断的因果方向可以 claim**: 训练时 trace 被截断导致模型学到的信息不完整 → 推理时即使看到完整 trace 也无法正确判断。模型能力差异是残余 confound（无法完全分离）。\\n\\n**Implications**: (1) 论文中所有 \\\"right-truncation corrupts/removes the verdict label\\\" 措辞对 VOTA 不成立——label 完整保留，被截断的是 trace INPUT content；(2) 14.96pp gap 中截断效应有因果证据（group_analysis），模型能力差异是 residual confound；(3) exception_only 因 token 长度短（远低于 2048）不受截断影响，其跨模型对比更 clean——进一步强化 Claim 1 \\\"most robust component\\\" 叙事；(4) 论文应讨论 max_length 的影响作为 limitation/future work；(5) group_analysis 数据可作为 Table/Figure 纳入论文——展示截断的因果效应。\\n\\n### D8: Data duplication as representation signature\\nexception_only 训练集 39.45% 重复（4720→2858 unique），测试集 48.99%（1190→607 unique），而 raw_traces 仅 1.81%（5910→5803 unique）。重复源自 exception_only 表征的 design-by-intent compactness：同一编程题的多个 passing 解执行时均无异常，exception_only 表征退化为相同的空标记。这一数据特征本身就是 Claim 3 two-tier hierarchy 的佐证——Tier 1 信号仅编码 fault presence/absence，不编码 solution-level details，因此天然高重复。Train-test input overlap = 0 排除数据泄漏。\\n\\n## Contingency Framing (已解决, 留存档)\\n\\n### Claim 1 — RESOLVED (D029: seed456=99.33% confirms \\\"consistently best component\\\")\\nseed456 与 seed42 完全一致（均 99.33%），seed123 为孤立离群值。Claim 1 定稿为 \\\"exception events consistently best component across models; absolute performance seed-dependent on DeepSeek (2/3 seeds robust, 1 outlier from LoRA init sensitivity); relative dominance over all alternatives robust across all seeds\\\"。\\\"0pp universal gap\\\" 叙事已放弃，转为数据驱动的 nuanced framing。\\n\\n### Claim 2 — RESOLVED (D006 + D009)\\nv5 full_trace 81.76%. plan_002 label-only 86.22%, gradient dilution = minor (26%). 跨模型 VOTA gap 补充证据.\\n\\n## 与 v1 Framing 的关键差异\\n\\n| 维度 | v1 | v2.9 |\\n|------|----|----|\\n| Headline | VOTA compression 优于 full trace | Exception events are the most effective diagnostic signal |\\n| Primary claim | VOTA +24pp through fault indicator preservation | Exception signal consistently best across models; absolute performance seed-dependent on DeepSeek |\\n| Claim 2 | Compression > full trace (只有 gradient dilution) | Information density primary + gradient dilution quantification + cross-model reinforcement |\\n| Claim 3 Tier 1 | exception_only only | exception + assertion-point (co-Tier 1, D020) |\\n| VOTA 定位 | 核心方法 — trace compression | **Fault indicator isolation** — 价值在于提取 exception, 非 generic compression |\\n| Model scope | 单模型 (Qwen) | 双模型, 跨模型对比是核心贡献 |\\n| DeepSeek full_trace | N/A | v1 collapsed (label truncation), v3 pending |\\n| Seed robustness | 未验证 | Qwen ✅ 99.83% ± 0.00 (3 seeds); DeepSeek ⚠️ seed-dependent (91-99%, D028) |\\n| Irreducible error | 未分析 | ✅ GeneratorExit 根因明确，99.83% = representation ceiling |\\n| Calibration | 未验证 | ✅ 完整 ECE+Brier, ~63x/~150x gap vs no_trace |\\n| Data duplication | 未分析 | ✅ exception_only ~40% 重复（representation compactness），train-test overlap = 0 |\\n| Early stopping | 未讨论 | DeepSeek epoch1→epoch3 轻微退化 (0.3-0.5pp); 核心问题是 LoRA 初始化 (seed123 epoch1=91.43%), 非 overfitting |\\n| Narrative arc | 1	\documentclass[11pt,a4paper]{article}
```
```text
2	\usepackage[review]{acl}

12	\title{Exception Events Are All You Need: \\Cross-Model Fault Detection for Verdict Prediction}
13	\author{Anonymous ACL submission}
14	
15	\begin{document}
16	\maketitle
17	
18	\begin{abstract}
19	Execution traces are increasingly used to enhance code language models, but which trace components matter for downstream tasks remains poorly understood.
20	We present the first systematic component ablation of trace representations for \emph{verdict prediction}---classifying whether a test passes or fails---across two models (Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B and DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B).
21	Our key finding is that exception events alone achieve near-perfect accuracy on Qwen (99.83\% $\pm$ 0.00, 3 seeds) and are the most effective component on DeepSeek (2/3 seeds at 99.33\%; 1 outlier at 91.09\% from LoRA initialization sensitivity), while a multi-component representation (VOTA) degrades by 14.96pp on the weaker model, confounded by training-time trace truncation.
22	We decompose the gap between full traces and compressed representations into gradient dilution (26\%) and information overload (74\%), establishing that irrelevant trace content---not training signal quality---is the primary bottleneck.
23	Component ablation reveals a two-tier diagnostic hierarchy: Tier~1 signals (exception events, assertion points) individually achieve ${\geq}$99.8\% accuracy, while Tier~2 signals (loop compression) reach only 85.6\%.
24	These results reframe verdict prediction as fault detection, where the minimal sufficient signal is a compact exception event that transfers well across model families; training stability is a secondary factor for smaller models.
25	\end{abstract}
26

32	\input{conclusion}
```

```text
1	\section{Introduction}
2	
3	Execution traces---line-by-line records of a program's runtime behavior---have emerged as a promising input modality for code language models.
4	Recent work has explored trace-augmented training for code generation \citep{chen2025tracepile}, execution-aware pretraining \citep{ding2024traced}, trace-based code understanding \citep{wang2025dcsh}, and self-training on trace chain-of-thought \citep{ni2024next}.
5	Yet a fundamental question remains open: \emph{which components of an execution trace actually matter?}
6	
7	\citet{wang2025dcsh} found that no single trace representation consistently outperforms others on generation-oriented tasks, concluding that the utility of execution traces is task- and format-dependent.
8	However, their study focused on code generation and repair---tasks that require the model to simulate or reconstruct execution semantics.
9	\emph{Verdict prediction}---classifying whether a test passes or fails---is a fundamentally different task type: it is a fault detection problem, not a generation problem.
10	Whether the same trace components matter for fault detection as for generation is unknown.
11	
12	We present the first systematic study of what trace components drive verdict prediction performance.
13	Using 591 problems from HumanEval \citep{chen2021evaluating} and MBPP-sanitized \citep{austin2021program} with model-generated solutions and execution traces, we decompose a multi-component trace representation (VOTA: exception events + assertion-point comparisons + loop compression) into individual components and evaluate each in isolation across two models: Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct and DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B-Instruct.
14	
15	Our results reveal a striking finding: \textbf{exception events alone achieve near-perfect verdict prediction on Qwen (99.83\% $\pm$ 0.00, 3 seeds) and are the most effective component on DeepSeek, with 2/3 seeds matching Qwen-level accuracy (99.33\%); the remaining seed (91.09\%) reflects LoRA initialization sensitivity rather than a signal limitation.}
16	Even the DeepSeek outlier seed (91.09\%) outperforms VOTA (84.45\%) and no-trace (73.36\%) by wide margins, confirming exception events as the dominant trace component across models.
17	In contrast, the full VOTA representation---which combines exception events with assertion-point and loop information---degrades by 14.96pp on DeepSeek.
18	Post-hoc analysis reveals the mechanism: the DeepSeek tokenizer produces longer sequences than Qwen for identical inputs, causing 91.7\% of VOTA training samples to exceed the 2{,}048-token context window; the training framework preserves the verdict label but truncates the trace content, so the model trains on incomplete diagnostic information.
19	
20	These results reframe verdict prediction as fault detection: the minimal sufficient signal is the exception event, a compact structured diagnostic moment that both models can interpret without deep trace reasoning.
21	We further decompose the gap between full trace and VOTA into two mechanisms---\emph{gradient dilution} (26\% of the gap) and \emph{information overload} (74\%)---showing that the primary bottleneck is not training signal quality but the presence of irrelevant trace content.
22	
23	We note that the finding that exception events dominate verdict prediction is not itself surprising---exceptions are, by definition, fault indicators.
24	The novelty lies in three less obvious results: (1)~adding more trace information actively \emph{hurts} performance (VOTA $<$ \texttt{exception\_only}), (2)~we quantify \emph{why} through a 26/74 gradient-dilution/information-overload decomposition, and (3)~the VOTA cross-model gap is confounded by tokenizer-driven trace truncation: the DeepSeek tokenizer causes the majority of VOTA training samples to exceed the context window (\S\ref{sec:cross_model}), truncating diagnostic trace content while preserving the verdict label.
25	
26	Our contributions are:
27	\begin{enumerate}
28	\item \textbf{Exception signal transfer}: We show that exception events alone achieve 99.83\% $\pm$ 0.00 on Qwen (3 seeds) and 99.33\% on 2/3 DeepSeek seeds, with one outlier at 91.09\% from LoRA initialization sensitivity; even the outlier exceeds VOTA (84.45\%) by 6.64pp (\S\ref{sec:cross_model}).
29	\item \textbf{Information density decomposition}: We decompose the full-trace-to-VOTA gap into gradient dilution (26\%) and information overload (74\%), identifying the primary bottleneck for trace-based prediction (\S\ref{sec:density}).
30	\item \textbf{Two-tier diagnostic hierarchy}: Component ablation reveals that Tier~1 signals (exception events, assertion points) individually achieve ${\geq}$99.8\% accuracy while Tier~2 signals (loop compression) reach only 85.6\% (\S\ref{sec:ablation}).
31	\end{enumerate}
```

```text
1	\section{Related Work}
2	
3	\paragraph{Execution Traces for Code LLMs.}
4	Several recent efforts have incorporated execution traces into code language model training.
5	TracePile \citep{chen2025tracepile} scaled trace-augmented training to 2.6M samples across multiple programming languages, demonstrating gains on code generation tasks.
6	\citet{wang2025dcsh} conducted a comprehensive study of execution trace-based information for code LLMs across synthesis, repair, and reasoning tasks, finding that trace-based semantic information has limited and inconsistent utility---a finding our work extends by showing that for \emph{verdict prediction}, exception events are a clear dominant signal.
7	NExT \citep{ni2024next} used self-training on execution trace chain-of-thought rationales, improving program repair on MBPP by 26.1\%.
8	ET-CoT \citep{eguchi2025etcot} introduced sub-line granularity traces via PyTracify, achieving 70.0\% on CRUXEval-O with SFT on Qwen2.5-7B.
9	\citet{jung2025execution} used debugger-extracted traces as grounded supervision for LLM reasoning, reducing overthinking while improving cross-domain transfer.
10	\citet{ferrando2025execute} compared regular, compact, and dynamic scratchpad strategies, finding that compact traces (recording only changed variables) perform comparably to full traces---directly supporting our finding that complete value information is redundant.
11	CRUXEval \citep{gu2024cruxeval} established benchmarks for code execution reasoning; CRUXEval-X \citep{xu2025cruxevalx} extended this to 19 programming languages.
12	A common limitation across these studies is that they focus on generation or output prediction tasks; none have decomposed which trace components contribute to \emph{classification} accuracy or tested cross-model generalization of trace signals.
13	
14	\paragraph{Code Verdict and Patch Classification.}
15	TRACED \citep{ding2024traced} showed that execution-aware pretraining improves vulnerability detection and clone detection, using encoder-based models with trace-augmented objectives.
16	In automated patch correctness prediction, APPT \citep{appt2024} fine-tuned pre-trained models with BERT+LSTM achieving 79.7\% accuracy on Defects4J patches, while LLM4PatchCorrect \citep{zhou2024llm4patch} used Starcoder-7B zero-shot with execution traces reaching 84.4\%.
17	More recently, \citet{yang2025apsg} proposed Graph-LoRA with attributed patch semantic graphs for parameter-efficient patch classification.
18	FuseFL \citep{widyasari2024fusefl} fused SBFL rankings with test execution outcomes (including error messages) for fault localization, demonstrating that structured fault signals improve over raw inputs.
19	\citet{foster2025mutation} deployed LLM-based equivalence detection at scale at Meta, achieving 0.95 precision on mutation testing.
20	These approaches treat the trace (or patch) as a monolithic input; no prior work has isolated individual trace components to determine their relative contribution to classification, or evaluated whether trace-based classifiers generalize across model families.
21	
22	\paragraph{Trace Compression and Selection.}
23	TraceCoder \citep{huang2026tracecoder} inserts diagnostic probes at selected locations rather than recording full traces, improving Pass@1 by 34.4\% for LLM-generated code debugging.
24	InspectCoder \citep{wang2025inspectcoder} enables LLMs to actively control a debugger for strategic breakpoint placement and runtime state inspection, outperforming baselines by 5--60\%.
25	ND-Slicer \citep{yadavally2024ndslicer} uses execution-aware pretraining to predict dynamic program slices without execution, achieving 81.3\% exact match.
26	\citet{haque2025traces} found that raw execution traces provide limited benefit for program repair and that LLM-optimized prompt formats consistently outperform raw traces---supporting our finding that trace compression is essential.
27	\citet{wu2026runtime} used selective variable snapshots in a multi-agent repair debate framework, further validating that targeted trace extraction outperforms full recording.
28	These methods target repair tasks (generating fixes) rather than classification, and none analyze the information density trade-off between trace completeness and prediction accuracy.
29	Our information density decomposition---separating gradient dilution (26\%) from information overload (74\%)---provides the first quantitative framework for understanding \emph{why} trace compression helps, complementing these methods' focus on \emph{how} to compress.
30	
```

```json
      "content": "\\section{Related Work}\n\n\\paragraph{Execution Traces for Code LLMs.}\nSeveral recent efforts have incorporated execution traces into code language model training.\nTracePile \\citep{chen2025tracepile} scaled trace-augmented training to 2.6M samples across multiple programming languages, demonstrating gains on code generation tasks.\n\\citet{wang2025dcsh} conducted a comprehensive study of execution trace-based information for code LLMs across synthesis, repair, and reasoning tasks, finding that trace-based semantic information has limited and inconsistent utility---a finding our work extends by showing that for \\emph{verdict prediction}, exception events are a clear dominant signal.\nNExT \\citep{ni2024next} used self-training on execution trace chain-of-thought rationales, improving program repair on MBPP by 26.1\\%.\nET-CoT \\citep{eguchi2025etcot} introduced sub-line granularity traces via PyTracify, achieving 70.0\\% on CRUXEval-O with SFT on Qwen2.5-7B.\n\\citet{jung2025execution} used debugger-extracted traces as grounded supervision for LLM reasoning, reducing overthinking while improving cross-domain transfer.\n\\citet{ferrando2025execute} compared regular, compact, and dynamic scratchpad strategies, finding that compact traces (recording only changed variables) perform comparably to full traces---directly supporting our finding that complete value information is redundant.\nCRUXEval \\citep{gu2024cruxeval} established benchmarks for code execution reasoning; CRUXEval-X \\citep{xu2025cruxevalx} extended this to 19 programming languages.\nA common limitation across these studies is that they focus on generation or output prediction tasks; none have decomposed which trace components contribute to \\emph{classification} accuracy or tested cross-model generalization of trace signals.\n\n\\paragraph{Code Verdict and Patch Classification.}\nTRACED \\citep{ding2024traced} showed that execution-aware pretraining improves vulnerability detection and clone detection, using encoder-based models with trace-augmented objectives.\nIn automated patch correctness prediction, APPT \\citep{appt2024} fine-tuned pre-trained models with BERT+LSTM achieving 79.7\\% accuracy on Defects4J patches, while LLM4PatchCorrect \\citep{zhou2024llm4patch} used Starcoder-7B zero-shot with execution traces reaching 84.4\\%.\nMore recently, \\citet{yang2025apsg} proposed Graph-LoRA with attributed patch semantic graphs for parameter-efficient patch classification.\nFuseFL \\citep{widyasari2024fusefl} fused SBFL rankings with test execution outcomes (including error messages) for fault localization, demonstrating that structured fault signals improve over raw inputs.\n\\citet{foster2025mutation} deployed LLM-based equivalence detection at scale at Meta, achieving 0.95 precision on mutation testing.\nThese approaches treat the trace (or patch) as a monolithic input; no prior work has isolated individual trace components to determine their relative contribution to classification, or evaluated whether trace-based classifiers generalize across model families.\n\n\\paragraph{Trace Compression and Selection.}\nTraceCoder \\citep{huang2026tracecoder} inserts diagnostic probes at selected locations rather than recording full traces, improving Pass@1 by 34.4\\% for LLM-generated code debugging.\nInspectCoder \\citep{wang2025inspectcoder} enables LLMs to actively control a debugger for strategic breakpoint placement and runtime state inspection, outperforming baselines by 5--60\\%.\nND-Slicer \\citep{yadavally2024ndslicer} uses execution-aware pretraining to predict dynamic program slices without execution, achieving 81.3\\% exact match.\n\\citet{haque2025traces} found that raw execution traces provide limited benefit for program repair and that LLM-optimized prompt formats consistently outperform raw traces---supporting our finding that trace compression is essential.\n\\citet{wu2026runtime} used selective variable snapshots in a multi-agent repair debate framework, further validating that targeted trace extraction outperforms full recording.\nThese methods target repair tasks (generating fixes) rather than classification, and none analyze the information density trade-off between trace completeness and prediction accuracy.\nOur information density decomposition---separating gradient dilution (26\\%) from information overload (74\\%)---provides the first quantitative framework for understanding \\emph{why} trace compression helps, complementing these methods' focus on \\emph{how} to compress.\n",
      "numLines": 30,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 30
    }
  }
}
```

```text
1	\section{Method}
2	
3	\subsection{Task Definition}
4	
5	We study \emph{verdict prediction}: given a Python function, a test input, and an optional execution trace, predict whether the test passes or fails.
6	The model produces a single token---\texttt{pass} or \texttt{fail}---making this a binary classification task.
7	Unlike code generation \citep{chen2025tracepile} or output prediction \citep{gu2024cruxeval}, verdict prediction isolates the model's ability to detect faults from its ability to simulate execution, providing a cleaner lens on what trace information actually contributes.
8	
9	\subsection{Dataset}
10	\label{sec:dataset}
11	
12	We sampled 10 candidate solutions per problem from Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct (temperature 0.8, top\_p 0.95) for 591 problems from HumanEval \citep{chen2021evaluating} (164 problems) and MBPP-sanitized \citep{austin2021program} (427 problems), executed each solution against canonical test cases with \texttt{sys.settrace} to obtain line-level execution traces and pass/fail verdicts, yielding 5,910 trace--verdict pairs (67\% pass, 33\% fail).
13	The dataset is split 80/20 by problem ID into 4,720 training and 1,190 test samples; the test set majority-class baseline is 71.51\%.
14	
15	\paragraph{Deduplication statistics.}
16	Because multiple passing solutions to the same problem produce identical (input, output) pairs in the \texttt{exception\_only} format---correct executions raise no exceptions, so the trace content is the same regardless of the solution's internal logic---the dataset contains duplicate rows.
17	Table~\ref{tab:dedup} reports the duplication rates across formats.
18	Train--test input overlap is zero (split by problem ID), so no information leakage occurs.
19	The high duplication in \texttt{exception\_only} does not affect our experimental conclusions: after deduplication the training set still contains 2,858 unique samples and the test set 607, providing sufficient coverage for the 591-problem benchmark.
20	The \texttt{exception\_only} test set contains 48.99\% duplicate inputs due to representation compactness; all confidence intervals treat samples as independent Bernoulli trials.
21	We report these statistics for transparency.
22	
23	\begin{table}[t]
24	\centering
25	\small
26	\begin{tabular}{lccc}
27	\toprule
28	\textbf{Split} & \textbf{Total} & \textbf{Unique} & \textbf{Dup.\ rate} \\
29	\midrule
30	exception\_only train & 4{,}720 & 2{,}858 & 39.45\% \\
31	exception\_only test  & 1{,}190 &   607 & 48.99\% \\
32	raw\_traces (full)    & 5{,}910 & 5{,}803 &  1.81\% \\
33	\bottomrule
34	\end{tabular}
35	\caption{Deduplication statistics. The \texttt{exception\_only} format collapses multiple passing solutions into identical rows because correct executions produce no exception events. Train--test input overlap is 0 (no leakage).}
36	\label{tab:dedup}
37	\end{table}
38	
39	\subsection{Trace Representations}
40	
41	We define seven input conditions that systematically vary the trace information available to the model (Figure~\ref{fig:trace_overview}):
42	
43	\begin{itemize}
44	\item \texttt{no\_trace}: Source code and test input only; no execution information.
45	\item \texttt{full\_trace}: Complete line-by-line execution trace recording variable states at each step.
46	\item \texttt{VOTA} (Verdict-Oriented Trace Abstraction): Three compressed components---exception events, assertion-point comparisons, and loop compression summaries.
47	\item \texttt{exception\_only}: Only exception and error events extracted from the trace (e.g., \texttt{[ERROR] TypeError: ...}).
48	\item \texttt{ae\_only}: Only assertion-point actual/expected value pairs from the final comparison.
49	\item \texttt{loop\_only}: Only loop compression summaries (iteration counts and final variable states).
50	\item \texttt{VOTA\_no\_ae}: VOTA without assertion-point information (exception events + loop compression).
51	\end{itemize}
52	
53	These conditions span a spectrum from no trace information (\texttt{no\_trace}) through maximally compressed signals (\texttt{exception\_only}) to the complete unfiltered trace (\texttt{full\_trace}).
54	
55	\begin{figure*}[t]
56	\centering
57	\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{fig1_trace_overview}
58	\caption{Overview of the verdict prediction pipeline. Source code and a test input are executed to produce a full raw trace. Four experimental conditions vary the trace information provided to the model: \texttt{no\_trace} (code only, 75.13\%), \texttt{full\_trace} (complete trace, 81.76\%), VOTA (compressed components, 99.41\%), and \texttt{exception\_only} (exception events only, 99.83\%). Each condition is paired with the same model to predict a pass/fail verdict. Accuracies shown are for Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B (seed=42, $N{=}1{,}190$).}
59	\label{fig:trace_overview}
60	\end{figure*}
61	
62	\subsection{Models}
63	
64	We evaluate two instruction-tuned code LLMs at the 7B parameter scale:
65	
66	\begin{itemize}
67	\item \textbf{Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct} \citep{qwen2025coder}: Pretrained on 5.5T tokens. Serves as the primary model for all ablation experiments.
68	\item \textbf{DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B-Instruct} \citep{deepseek2024coder}: Pretrained on 2T tokens. Used for cross-model validation to test whether findings generalize beyond a single model family.
69	\end{itemize}
70	
71	The two models differ primarily in pretraining data volume (5.5T vs.\ 2T tokens), providing a natural axis for evaluating whether trace signal utility is model-dependent \citep{dong2024composition}.
72	
73	\subsection{Training Setup}
74	
75	All conditions use identical training configurations to ensure fair comparison.
76	We apply LoRA \citep{hu2022lora} (rank=16, $\alpha$=32, dropout=0.05) to all linear layers and train for 3 epochs with batch size 4, gradient accumulation 8 (effective batch size 32), learning rate $2 \times 10^{-4}$ with linear warmup and linear decay, warmup ratio 0.1, and weight decay 0.01.
77	The maximum sequence length is 2,048 tokens; input sequences exceeding this limit are truncated from the input content while preserving the complete verdict label.
78	Training wall-clock time ranges from 0.5 to 2.5 hours per condition on a single GPU, depending on trace format verbosity.
79	The primary seed is 42; seed robustness is evaluated with seeds 123 and 456 for the \texttt{exception\_only} condition.
80	
81	\subsection{Loss Ablation}
82	
83	To disentangle training signal quality from input signal quality, we introduce a \texttt{full\_trace\_label\_only} variant.
84	This condition uses the same full trace as input but computes loss only on the verdict token, masking all trace tokens during training (loss weight = 0 on trace positions).
85	If this variant improves over standard \texttt{full\_trace}, the improvement is attributable to \emph{gradient dilution}---trace tokens diluting the training signal without contributing to verdict prediction.
86	The remaining gap to VOTA is then attributable to \emph{information overload}---irrelevant trace content that the model must process at inference time.
```

```text
1	\section{Experiments}
2	
3	We evaluate our trace preprocessing pipeline across two research questions:
4	(1)~Do Tier~1 exception signals generalize across models of different capability?
5	(2)~What is the relative contribution of each trace component to verdict prediction accuracy?
6	All experiments use our trace--verdict dataset (\S\ref{sec:dataset}) with $N{=}1{,}190$ test samples; accuracy is reported with Wilson 95\% confidence intervals unless stated otherwise.
7	Pairwise comparisons use paired bootstrap resampling ($B{=}10{,}000$, seed=42); all cross-condition differences reported below are significant at $p{<}0.0001$ unless noted otherwise.
8	
9	\subsection{Cross-Model Comparison}
10	\label{sec:cross_model}
11	
12	To test whether exception-based trace compression transfers across model families, we compare Qwen-2.5-7B-Instruct and DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B-Instruct under four input conditions: \texttt{exception\_only} (Tier~1 exception events), VOTA (exception + assertion-point + loop compression), \texttt{full\_trace} (complete execution trace), and \texttt{no\_trace} (source code only).
13	
14	\begin{table}[t]
15	\centering
16	\small
17	\begin{tabular}{lccc}
18	\toprule
19	\textbf{Condition} & \textbf{Qwen-2.5-7B} & \textbf{DeepSeek-6.7B} & \textbf{Gap} \\
20	\midrule
21	exception\_only & \textbf{99.83}\% & 99.33\%$^\dag$ & 0.50pp \\
22	VOTA & 99.41\% & 84.45\% & 14.96pp \\
23	VOTA\_no\_ae & 99.41\%$^\S$ & --- & --- \\
24	full\_trace & 81.76\% & N/A$^\ddag$ & --- \\
25	no\_trace & 75.13\% & 73.36\% & 1.77pp \\
26	\midrule
27	Majority baseline & 71.51\% & 71.51\% & --- \\
28	\bottomrule
29	\end{tabular}
30	\caption{Cross-model accuracy ($N{=}1{,}190$). Qwen CIs: exception\_only [99.58, 100.00], VOTA [98.82, 99.83], full\_trace [79.70, 83.90], no\_trace [72.77, 77.48]; DeepSeek CIs: VOTA [82.60, 86.40], no\_trace [70.80, 75.70]. $^\dag$DeepSeek \texttt{exception\_only} 3-seed results: 99.33\% (seeds 42, 456; CI [98.82, 99.75]) and 91.09\% (seed 123; CI [89.40, 92.60]); seed 123 is a LoRA initialization outlier (fail recall = 70.5\% vs.\ 100\%). $^\S$VOTA\_no\_ae evaluated on Qwen only; CI [98.90, 99.80]. $^\ddag$DeepSeek full\_trace was invalidated because the DeepSeek tokenizer produced sequences exceeding 2{,}048 tokens, causing the verdict label to be truncated during training; the model consequently failed to learn the classification task.}
31	\label{tab:cross_model}
32	\end{table}
33	
34	Table~\ref{tab:cross_model} reveals a striking asymmetry.
35	Under \texttt{exception\_only}, Qwen achieves 99.83\% $\pm$ 0.00 across three seeds, while DeepSeek shows a bimodal pattern: seeds 42 and 456 both achieve 99.33\% (gap = 0.50pp), while seed 123 yields 91.09\% (gap = 8.74pp), attributable to suboptimal LoRA initialization (fail recall = 70.5\% vs.\ 100\% for other seeds).
36	Even the outlier seed outperforms VOTA (84.45\%) by 6.64pp, confirming exception events as the most effective component across models.
37	Under VOTA, the gap widens to 14.96pp: DeepSeek drops to 84.45\% while Qwen remains at 99.41\%.
38	Post-hoc tokenizer analysis reveals a confound: measured on the training set using each model's tokenizer, the DeepSeek tokenizer averages 2{,}759 tokens per VOTA sample versus 1{,}908 for Qwen, causing 91.7\% of DeepSeek VOTA training samples to exceed the 2{,}048-token context window.
39	Unlike the DeepSeek \texttt{full\_trace} condition (Table~\ref{tab:cross_model}, $^\ddag$), where right-truncation removed the verdict label entirely, the VOTA training framework preserves the verdict label but truncates the trace content (input), so the model trains with incomplete diagnostic information for the vast majority of the dataset.
40	The 14.96pp gap thus reflects both model capability differences and training-time trace truncation; the two factors cannot be disentangled in the current design.
41	However, a post-hoc group analysis provides directional evidence: partitioning DeepSeek VOTA test samples by whether their training-set counterparts exceeded the context window, intact samples ($N{=}99$) achieve 100\% accuracy while truncated samples ($N{=}1{,}091$) achieve 83.04\% (95\% CI [80.75\%, 85.24\%]; Fisher exact $p{<}0.001$), with all 185 errors concentrated in the fail class of the truncated group (fail recall $=$ 31.23\%).
42	In contrast, the \texttt{exception\_only} condition is unaffected by truncation because exception-based inputs are short enough to fit within the context window for both tokenizers, making its cross-model comparison clean.
43	Per-class analysis (Appendix~\ref{sec:per_class}) reveals the mechanism: DeepSeek VOTA achieves 100\% fail precision but only 45.43\% fail recall, systematically defaulting to \texttt{pass}---in contrast to Qwen VOTA, which achieves 100\% fail recall.
44	
45	The \texttt{no\_trace} baseline confirms that source code alone provides limited signal (75.13\% for Qwen, 73.36\% for DeepSeek), only marginally above majority-class guessing (71.51\%).
46	
47	Seed robustness diverges sharply across models.
48	Qwen \texttt{exception\_only} produces identical accuracy of 99.83\% across all three seeds ($\sigma{=}0$, $n{=}3$, exactly two errors each).
49	DeepSeek shows a bimodal pattern ($n{=}3$): seeds 42 and 456 both achieve 99.33\% (8 errors each, fail recall = 100\%), while seed 123 yields 91.09\% (fail recall = 70.5\%), consistent with LoRA initialization sensitivity on the smaller model.
50	Because the \texttt{exception\_only} test set contains 48.99\% duplicate inputs (607 unique out of 1,190; \S\ref{sec:dataset}), the reported CIs assume $n{=}1{,}190$ independent trials.
51	Under deduplication ($n{=}607$), Qwen's two errors yield 99.67\% accuracy (Wilson CI [98.81, 99.91]), confirming that the result is robust to the effective sample size.
52	
53	\subsection{Component Ablation}
54	\label{sec:ablation}
55	
56	We decompose the VOTA pipeline into individual components on Qwen-2.5-7B (seed=42).
57	Table~\ref{tab:ablation} arranges conditions into a two-tier hierarchy: Tier~1 signals (\texttt{ae\_only}, \texttt{exception\_only}) that individually achieve ${\geq}$99.8\% accuracy, and Tier~2 signals (\texttt{loop\_only}) that provide moderate improvement over the no-trace baseline.
58	
59	\begin{table}[t]
60	\centering
61	\small
62	\begin{tabular}{llccc}
63	\toprule
64	\textbf{Condition} & \textbf{Components} & \textbf{Acc (\%)} & \textbf{95\% CI} & \textbf{Tier} \\
65	\midrule
66	ae\_only & assertion-point & \textbf{99.92} & [99.75, 100.00] & 1 \\
67	exception\_only & exception events & 99.83 & [99.58, 100.00] & 1 \\
68	VOTA & exc+ae+loop & 99.41 & [98.82, 99.83] & --- \\
69	VOTA\_no\_ae & exc+loop & 99.41 & [98.90, 99.80] & --- \\
70	loop\_only & loop compression & 85.63 & [83.53, 87.56] & 2 \\
71	full\_trace & complete trace & 81.76 & [79.70, 83.90] & --- \\
72	no\_trace & none & 75.13 & [72.77, 77.48] & --- \\
73	\bottomrule
74	\end{tabular}
75	\caption{Component ablation on Qwen-2.5-7B ($N{=}1{,}190$, seed=42). Tier~1 signals individually achieve near-perfect accuracy; combining them (VOTA) slightly dilutes performance.}
76	\label{tab:ablation}
77	\end{table}
78	
79	Two findings stand out.
80	First, the Tier~1 signals are individually sufficient: \texttt{ae\_only} (99.92\%) and \texttt{exception\_only} (99.83\%) are not distinguishable ($p{=}0.80$, paired bootstrap, $B{=}10{,}000$), and both exceed VOTA (99.41\%, $p{=}0.01$ for \texttt{exception\_only} vs.\ VOTA, paired bootstrap).
81	This implies that combining signals in VOTA introduces minor noise rather than complementary information.
82	Second, removing assertion points from VOTA has no effect (VOTA\_no\_ae = VOTA = 99.41\%), confirming that within the VOTA mixture, exception events dominate the prediction signal.
83	
84	Tier~2 signal \texttt{loop\_only} (85.63\%) improves over \texttt{no\_trace} (75.13\%) by 10.50pp ($p{<}0.0001$, bootstrap 95\% CI $[{+}8.24, {+}12.86]$), showing that loop compression captures some execution semantics, but far less than exception events.
85	
86	\subsection{Information Density Decomposition}
87	\label{sec:density}
88	
89	The gap between \texttt{full\_trace} (81.76\%) and VOTA (99.41\%) is 17.65pp.
90	We decompose this gap into two mechanisms by introducing an intermediate condition, \texttt{full\_trace\_label\_only}, which retains the complete trace as input but computes loss only on the verdict token, masking all trace tokens during training.
91	
92	\begin{table}[t]
93	\centering
94	\small
95	\begin{tabular}{lccc}
96	\toprule
97	\textbf{Condition} & \textbf{Acc (\%)} & \textbf{$\Delta$ vs.\ full\_trace} & \textbf{Gap explained} \\
98	\midrule
99	full\_trace & 81.76 & --- & --- \\
100	label\_only & 86.22 & +4.46pp & 26\% \\
101	VOTA & 99.41 & +17.65pp & 100\% \\
102	\bottomrule
103	\end{tabular}
104	\caption{Information density decomposition. The full\_trace$\to$VOTA gap decomposes into gradient dilution (26\%, addressed by masking trace tokens from the loss) and information overload (74\%, addressed by removing irrelevant trace lines via VOTA compression).}
105	\label{tab:density}
106	\end{table}
107	
108	As shown in Table~\ref{tab:density}, the decomposition attributes 26\% of the gap (+4.46pp, $p{<}0.0001$, bootstrap 95\% CI $[{+}2.35, {+}6.55]$) to \emph{gradient dilution}---the training signal is diluted across trace tokens that do not contribute to verdict prediction---and 74\% (+13.19pp) to \emph{information overload}---the model must process trace lines irrelevant to the fault at inference time.
109	This two-factor decomposition is consistent across models: the VOTA cross-model gap (14.96pp, Table~\ref{tab:cross_model}) is confounded by training-time trace truncation on DeepSeek (\S\ref{sec:cross_model}), which amplifies information overload by removing diagnostic trace content from the majority of training samples.
110	
111	\subsection{Calibration Analysis}
112	\label{sec:calibration}
113	
114	Beyond accuracy, we evaluate whether trace conditions affect prediction calibration, motivated by recent findings that code LLM confidence is often poorly calibrated \citep{spiess2025calibration}.
115	Figure~\ref{fig:calibration} shows reliability diagrams for four conditions on Qwen-2.5-7B.
116	
117	\begin{table}[t]
118	\centering
119	\small
120	\begin{tabular}{lcc}
121	\toprule
122	\textbf{Condition} & \textbf{ECE $\downarrow$} & \textbf{Brier $\downarrow$} \\
123	\midrule
124	ae\_only & \textbf{0.0004} & \textbf{0.0008} \\
125	exception\_only & 0.003 & 0.0013 \\
126	VOTA & 0.005 & 0.005 \\
127	full\_trace & 0.096 & 0.144 \\
128	no\_trace & 0.171 & 0.197 \\
129	\bottomrule
130	\end{tabular}
131	\caption{Calibration metrics on Qwen-2.5-7B ($N{=}1{,}190$, seed=42). ECE = expected calibration error; Brier = Brier score. Lower is better for both.}
132	\label{tab:calibration}
133	\end{table}
134	
135	Table~\ref{tab:calibration} shows that calibration quality mirrors the Tier~1/2 hierarchy.
136	Tier~1 conditions (\texttt{ae\_only}: ECE=0.0004; \texttt{exception\_only}: ECE=0.003) produce near-perfectly calibrated predictions, while \texttt{full\_trace} (ECE=0.096) and \texttt{no\_trace} (ECE=0.171) exhibit substantial miscalibration.
137	VOTA (ECE=0.005) is well-calibrated but slightly worse than either Tier~1 component alone, reinforcing the pattern that combining trace signals dilutes rather than improves the prediction signal.
138	
139	The Brier scores tell a consistent story: \texttt{ae\_only} (0.0008) and \texttt{exception\_only} (0.0013) achieve near-zero scores, while \texttt{no\_trace} (0.197) indicates substantial miscalibration.
140	
141	Figure~\ref{fig:cross_model_gap} visualizes the cross-model gap pattern, showing that exception-based conditions maintain tight cross-model agreement while VOTA and lower-tier conditions diverge.
142	
143	\begin{figure}[t]
144	\centering
145	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{fig2_calibration}
146	\caption{Reliability diagrams for four trace conditions on Qwen-2.5-7B ($N{=}1{,}190$, seed=42). The Tier~1 condition (\texttt{exception\_only}) clusters near the diagonal (well-calibrated), while \texttt{full\_trace} and \texttt{no\_trace} exhibit systematic overconfidence.}
147	\label{fig:calibration}
148	\end{figure}
149	
150	\begin{figure}[t]
151	\centering
152	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{fig3_cross_model_gap}
153	\caption{Cross-model accuracy gap (Qwen $-$ DeepSeek) across trace conditions. Exception-based signals transfer across models (0.50pp gap), while VOTA is model-dependent (14.96pp gap).}
154	\label{fig:cross_model_gap}
155	\end{figure}
```

```text
1	\section{Discussion}
2	
3	\subsection{Why Exception Events Dominate}
4	
5	Verdict prediction is fundamentally a fault detection task: the model must determine whether execution deviated from expected behavior.
6	Exception events are the most explicit signal of such deviation---structured diagnostic moments that encode the nature and location of a fault.
7	Unlike loop compressions or assertion-point values, which require the model to integrate information across multiple trace elements, exception events provide a self-contained verdict cue.
8	This explains why \texttt{exception\_only} matches or exceeds VOTA: adding non-exception trace components dilutes the model's attention on the diagnostic signal rather than providing complementary information.
9	
10	We acknowledge that exception-based verdict prediction is, at its core, fault indicator presence detection: the model learns to map the presence or absence of a structured error message to a binary label, which is close to trivially solvable for any instruction-tuned LLM.
11	The Tier~1 accuracy of ${\geq}$99.8\% is therefore unsurprising in isolation.
12	The contribution of this finding lies not in demonstrating that a strong signal yields high accuracy, but in two less obvious results: (a)~adding more trace information---loop compressions, assertion-point values---actively \emph{hurts} performance (VOTA $<$ \texttt{exception\_only}, Table~\ref{tab:ablation}), quantifying an information density dilution effect that has not been previously measured; and (b)~this dilution is confounded by tokenizer-driven trace truncation on DeepSeek: the DeepSeek tokenizer produces longer sequences than Qwen for identical inputs, causing the majority of VOTA training samples to exceed the context window (\S\ref{sec:cross_model}); the verdict label is preserved, but the trace content is truncated, depriving the model of diagnostic information and contributing to the collapse of DeepSeek VOTA fail recall to 45.43\% (Table~\ref{tab:per_class}) while Qwen maintains 100\%.
13	
14	\subsection{Simplicity as Relative Robustness}
15	
16	The cross-model gap pattern (Table~\ref{tab:cross_model}) suggests a design principle: \emph{trace representations should match the model capability floor, not the ceiling.}
17	Exception events are consistently the most effective component on both models, and their structural simplicity---a fixed-format error message---ensures that the relative advantage over richer representations holds across all seeds.
18	Two of three DeepSeek seeds match Qwen-level accuracy (99.33\%); the outlier (seed 123, 91.09\%) is attributable to LoRA initialization landing in a suboptimal basin (fail recall = 70.5\%), not to a failure of the exception signal itself---even this outlier outperforms VOTA (84.45\%) by 6.64pp.
19	The VOTA cross-model gap is confounded by two factors: (1)~tokenizer efficiency---the DeepSeek tokenizer averages 2{,}759 tokens per VOTA sample versus Qwen's 1{,}908, causing 91.7\% of training samples to exceed the 2{,}048-token context window, truncating trace content while preserving the verdict label---and (2)~base model capability differences, consistent with findings that SFT generalization depends on pretrain quality \citep{yang2024generalization}.
20	The two factors cannot be disentangled in the current experimental design.
21	Our \texttt{exception\_only} design sidesteps the truncation confound entirely by keeping inputs short (median 314 tokens), making its cross-model comparison clean and the ``capability floor'' principle directly testable.
22	The practical implication is that trace-based systems should default to the simplest sufficient signal; for smaller models, training stability (multi-seed selection, warmup schedules) is a secondary but addressable concern.
23	
24	\subsection{Training Dynamics and Loss Decoupling}
25	
26	The DeepSeek \texttt{exception\_only} results reveal two instructive training dynamics.
27	First, epoch-level analysis shows mild degradation in 2/3 seeds: seed 42 peaks at 99.83\% after epoch~1 but settles at 99.33\% by epoch~3 ($-$0.50pp), seed 123 degrades from 91.43\% to 91.09\% ($-$0.34pp), while seed 456 shows no epoch degradation (99.33\% at both epoch~1 and epoch~3).
28	Early stopping may yield marginal gains for some seeds, but the epoch effect is small (${\leq}$0.5pp) compared to the seed effect (8.24pp between seeds 42 and 123).
29	
30	Second, seeds 123 and 456 converge to nearly identical final training losses (0.122 and 0.123 respectively), yet their accuracies differ by 8.24pp (91.09\% vs.\ 99.33\%).
31	The critical difference lies in fail recall: seeds 42 and 456 achieve 100\%, while seed 123 achieves only 70.5\%---the LoRA initialization in seed 123 fails to learn the exception$\to$fail mapping for a substantial fraction of failing samples, even though the per-token loss appears converged.
32	This demonstrates that token-level training loss can mask fundamental differences in sample-level classification ability, suggesting a parallel to the eval-loss/accuracy decoupling observed in DeepSeek VOTA (where eval loss rose 65\% from epoch 1 to 3 while accuracy improved by 6.3pp) at the training phase.
33	The practical recommendation is twofold: (1)~use task-specific metrics (accuracy, fail recall) for checkpoint and seed selection rather than training or evaluation loss, and (2)~for deployment on smaller models, train multiple seeds and select the best, as a single run may not reliably converge to the correct classification boundary.
34	
35	The contrast with VOTA is instructive: DeepSeek VOTA accuracy monotonically increases from 78.15\% to 84.45\% across epochs, but is consistent with a performance limit associated with the high trace truncation rate (\S\ref{sec:cross_model})---the model progressively extracts signal from samples with intact trace content but cannot fully compensate for the information loss in the truncated majority.
36	The 84.45\% accuracy reflects a combination of trace information loss and model capability constraints.
37	
38	\subsection{Error Analysis}
39	\label{sec:error_analysis}
40	
41	Seed robustness testing revealed that the two errors made by \texttt{exception\_only} (accuracy = 99.83\%, 2/1190 incorrect) are \emph{identical across all three seeds} (42, 123, 456).
42	Both errors are false negatives on samples involving the \texttt{check\_dict\_case} function (sample IDs 901 and 905): the ground truth is \texttt{pass}, but the model predicts \texttt{fail}.
43	This 100\% error overlap ($\sigma{=}0$) across seeds demonstrates that these are not stochastic training artifacts but \emph{irreducible samples} at the boundary of what exception-based trace signals can resolve.
44	
45	Manual inspection reveals the root cause: \texttt{check\_dict\_case} (HumanEval/95) uses \texttt{all()} over a generator expression, and Python's \texttt{all()} short-circuits on the first \texttt{True} value, raising \texttt{GeneratorExit} to terminate the generator.
46	Because \texttt{GeneratorExit} is a \texttt{BaseException} subclass, the tracer records it as seven \texttt{[ERROR] GeneratorExit} lines---even though the function executes correctly and the test passes.
47	The model observes these spurious error markers and reasonably but incorrectly predicts \texttt{fail}.
48	This is not a model failure but a \emph{representation ceiling}: the \texttt{exception\_only} format cannot distinguish genuine exceptions from benign \texttt{BaseException} subclasses triggered by Python's iterator protocol.
49	The 99.83\% accuracy is therefore a deterministic upper bound for this trace representation on this dataset, not an indication of insufficient model capacity.
50	
51	\subsection{Limitations}
52	
53	Our study has several limitations.
54	First, all experiments use a single dataset (591 problems from HumanEval and MBPP-sanitized); generalization to other languages, larger codebases, or different fault types remains untested.
55	Second, we evaluate only two models at the 7B parameter scale; the relationship between model size and trace signal utility is unexplored.
56	Third, DeepSeek \texttt{exception\_only} exhibits seed-dependent variance: 2/3 seeds achieve 99.33\% while seed 123 yields 91.09\% ($\sigma{=}4.76$pp, $n{=}3$), attributable to LoRA initialization sensitivity (fail recall = 70.5\% for the outlier vs.\ 100\% for others).
57	Qwen shows perfect seed robustness ($\sigma{=}0$, $n{=}3$).
58	Whether training stabilization techniques (e.g., larger warmup ratio, LoRA rank adjustment) can eliminate DeepSeek's seed sensitivity is unexplored.
59	Other conditions use a single seed.
60	Fourth, the DeepSeek \texttt{full\_trace} condition was invalidated because the DeepSeek tokenizer produced sequences exceeding 2,048 tokens, causing the verdict label to be truncated during training; the model consequently failed to learn the classification task, and we report it as N/A rather than presenting potentially misleading results.
61	Fifth, the candidate solutions were generated by Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct, which also serves as one of our evaluation models.
62	This does not affect verdict prediction---the task is to classify a \emph{given} solution as pass or fail, not to generate solutions---but we note the overlap for transparency.
63	Moreover, the pass/fail distribution in the dataset is determined by the generating model's coding ability; a stronger or weaker generator would yield a different class balance, potentially affecting task difficulty and the generalizability of our accuracy estimates.
64	Finally, our analysis is limited to verdict prediction; whether exception dominance extends to other classification tasks (vulnerability detection, clone detection) or to generation tasks (program repair) is an open question.
```

```text
1	\section{Conclusion}
2	
3	We presented the first systematic study of what execution trace components drive verdict prediction performance.
4	Through component ablation across two code LLMs, we established three findings:
5	
6	\textbf{(1) Exception signal transfer.}
7	Exception events alone achieve 99.83\% $\pm$ 0.00 on Qwen (3 seeds) and 99.33\% on 2/3 DeepSeek seeds, with one outlier at 91.09\% from LoRA initialization sensitivity.
8	Even the outlier exceeds VOTA (84.45\%) by 6.64pp, confirming exception events as the most effective component across models; training stability is a secondary factor for smaller models.
9	In contrast, the full VOTA representation degrades by 14.96pp on DeepSeek---a gap confounded by training-time trace truncation (the majority of DeepSeek VOTA samples exceed the context window; \S\ref{sec:cross_model}) and model capability differences, which cannot be disentangled in the current design.
10	
11	\textbf{(2) Information density decomposition.}
12	The 17.65pp gap between full traces and VOTA decomposes into gradient dilution (26\%, from training on irrelevant trace tokens) and information overload (74\%, from processing irrelevant trace lines at inference).
13	The primary bottleneck is trace content selection, not loss design.
14	
15	\textbf{(3) Two-tier diagnostic hierarchy.}
16	Tier~1 signals (exception events, assertion points) individually achieve ${\geq}$99.8\% accuracy, while Tier~2 signals (loop compression) reach 85.6\%.
17	Combining tiers in VOTA slightly degrades performance (99.41\%), confirming that additional trace components introduce noise rather than complementary signal.
18	
19	These results yield a practical design principle: for verdict prediction, extract the highest-specificity diagnostic signal and discard everything else.
20	Future work should test whether exception dominance extends to other datasets (DebugBench, LiveCodeBench), model scales, and classification tasks beyond verdict prediction.
```

```text
100	\section{VOTA Trace Examples}
101	\label{sec:vota_examples}
102	
103	We provide two representative VOTA trace examples illustrating the fail and pass cases.
104	In the fail case, the exception event provides an unambiguous fault signal.
105	In the pass case, the absence of an exception event combined with a matching assertion point confirms correct execution.
106	
107	\paragraph{Fail example.}
108	A \texttt{ZeroDivisionError} triggers an \texttt{[ERROR]} event, providing a direct fault signal that is sufficient for the model to predict \texttt{fail}.
109	
110	\begin{small}
111	\begin{verbatim}
112	## Function
113	def divide(a, b):
114	    return a / b
115	
116	## Test Input
117	divide(1, 0)
118	
119	## Expected Output
120	inf
121	
122	## VOTA Trace
123	[ERROR] ZeroDivisionError: division by zero
124	
125	## Verdict
126	fail
127	\end{verbatim}
128	\end{small}
129	
130	\paragraph{Pass example.}
131	No exception is raised, and the assertion point confirms that the actual output matches the expected value.
132	
133	\begin{small}
134	\begin{verbatim}
135	## Function
136	def add(a, b):
137	    return a + b
138	
139	## Test Input
140	add(2, 3)
141	
142	## Expected Output
143	5
144	
145	## VOTA Trace
146	[LOOP] No loops detected.
147	[ASSERT] actual=5, expected=5
148	
149	## Verdict
150	pass
151	\end{verbatim}
152	\end{small}
153	
154	These examples illustrate the core asymmetry exploited by the \texttt{exception\_only} condition: fail cases produce an explicit \texttt{[ERROR]} event, while pass cases are characterized by its absence.
155	This binary signal---presence vs.\ absence of an exception---is sufficient for near-perfect verdict prediction.
156	
157	%% ============================================================
```
